### Offerings surfacing at one sharing

Exhibit ONE’s **Offerings to one sharing, surfacing** and THIRTY’s resolver statements agree in sequence.

An offered `0` supplies no parity. The first nonzero offering surfaces its parity. A further offering at that parity leaves it. An offering at the opposite parity makes the surfacing `0`. Once that disagreement has occurred, further offerings cannot restore either parity at this receiving.

| Offerings at this one sharing | Surfacing |
|---|---|
| No nonzero offering | None |
| One or more `+`, no `−` | `+` |
| One or more `−`, no `+` | `−` |
| Both `+` and `−`, in either order | `0` |

Thus `0, +` surfaces `+`, whereas `+, −, +` surfaces `0`. Repetition after disagreement supplies no majority. The distinction between an offered `0` and the `0` reached by disagreement matters to the order of this derivation. Neither is a third parity.

This result concerns the offerings at the stated receiving. It does not decide the further question of which along and across arrivals constitute the two sequential parities of a full crossing.

### The self at its carried sharing

At ONE’s **One self, one momentary**, let `p` stand for either published prior parity, and `−p` for its opposite. These symbols denote the two cases in the stable form.

| What surfaces | Shared at 10 | Carried next at 11 |
|---|---|---|
| The same parity `p` | `0` | `p` |
| The opposite parity `−p` | `−p` | `−p` |
| None | `−p` | `−p` |
| `0` from disagreeing offerings | `−p` | `−p` |

Taking `p` at `+` and at `−` gives all eight cells. THIRTY’s **A self’s next, one momentary at a time**, statements 623–624, follows the same cases.

Two consequences carry the reasoning onward. First, a nonzero shared parity is also the self’s next parity. Second, when one nonzero parity surfaces, it is the next parity whether the self’s prior agrees or differs. Agreement and difference reach the same next parity through different branches; their shared outputs remain different.

The shared output and the continuing carrying must therefore remain separate subjects. A shared `0` leaves the carrying continuing. A `0` arriving from the other surfaces none, at which the receiving self inverts its own prior in this bounded case. The first occurrence does not erase the second self’s own changing.

ONE’s released output arrives at the receiving self’s next receiving. Each self’s next carrying continues as its own prior there. Following the two selves in a different reading order adds no earlier arrival inside the published grouped transition.

### Two selves followed through the openings

The conditions are one sharing; a parity already carried at each self; coupling both ways; no offerings from beyond the two; and nothing arriving at the first momentary. Every later arrival here is the other’s preceding shared or released output.

There are four binary openings. They are exhausted by an alike opening and an opposite opening, each also read with all signs inverted.

**Alike opening.** Both begin at `p`. Nothing arrives, so both share `−p` and continue at `−p`. Each then receives the other’s `−p`, agrees, shares `0` and continues at `−p`. The arriving `0` next surfaces none, so both share `p` and continue at `p`. Each then receives `p`, agrees, shares `0` and continues at `p`. The same conditions recur. Their shared sequence is therefore `−p, 0, p, 0, …`.

**Opposite opening.** One begins at `p`, the other at `−p`. With nothing arriving, each first inverts. At the next receiving, each receives the other’s preceding release, opposite its own carrying. Each inverts again. This preserves the opposite relation and the same reasoning continues. Every shared output is nonzero, and each self alternates.

Both openings follow directly from the local binary. No independently chosen intermediate carrying has been inserted into either history.

### The five parity relation and its intervening step

ONE’s **Two selves, three momentaries, five parities** gives the following places, in this order:

1. The other’s prior carrying.
2. The other’s shared output.
3. The receiving self’s now carrying.
4. The receiving self’s shared output.
5. The receiving self’s next carrying.

The first and fifth belong to different selves at different positions in the sequence. Their relation can be proved through both possibilities for the other’s shared output.

**If the other shares a nonzero parity:** with prior `p`, its shared output is `−p`. That arrives at the receiving self. The local result above makes the receiver’s next `−p`, whether the receiver now agrees with that arriving parity or differs from it.

**If the other shares `0`:** an intervening step is necessary. After any nonzero release `p`, that self carries `p`, so its next release can be `0` or `−p`, never another nonzero `p`. Therefore consecutive nonzero releases cannot be equal. A self here cannot release `0` twice consecutively: two consecutive agreements would require its one predecessor to release the same nonzero parity consecutively. The opening, with none arriving, cannot release `0` either.

Consequently the other’s `0` was immediately preceded by its own nonzero release. Because the other now carries `p`, that preceding release was `p`. It arrived at the receiver’s preceding receiving and made the receiver’s now `p`. The other’s `0` now arrives, surfaces none, and the receiver inverts to `−p`.

Thus both branches give the self’s next as the other’s prior inverted. The three forms at `p`, each read at both parities, are precisely the six published rows:

| Other prior | Other shared | Self now | Self shared | Self next |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `+` | `−` | `−` | `0` | `−` |
| `+` | `−` | `+` | `−` | `−` |
| `+` | `0` | `+` | `−` | `−` |
| `−` | `+` | `+` | `0` | `+` |
| `−` | `+` | `−` | `+` | `+` |
| `−` | `0` | `−` | `+` | `+` |

The six belong to the four openings together, from the second momentary. An alike opening visits four forms; an opposite opening visits two. ONE’s carrying already receives this exact scope from **For The Managing**, twenty-fourth part. This pass supplies a checked sequential derivation of an already carried result.

The first draft of this review skipped the intervening receiving in the `0` branch. The completed proof above repairs that omission. Agreement at the ending alone would not have justified the draft.

### THIRTY followed from its finite binary premises

In **Next from prior and now, the right spiral step**, THIRTY explicitly specifies the transition from `(prior, now)` to `(now, next)`. Let the two prior places be `p` and `n`. A binary next is selected for each of four input pairs, giving sixteen functions.

For the finite statement that the prior is preserved, the exact condition used here is: **at a fixed now, different priors give different nexts**. Pairs with different nows already remain distinct in the first output place. Pairs with the same now remain distinct precisely when their nexts differ. There are two permitted choices at each of two nows, giving four functions; the remaining twelve merge at least two joint forms.

Following the four functions yields this complete classification. In the comparison rows, `+` denotes agreement for “agreeing” and difference for “parting”; reversing this convention exchanges those two functions without changing the conclusion.

| Next is | Joint forms remaining at themselves | Other joint forms followed in sequence |
|---|---|---|
| Prior | `++`, `−−` | `+− → −+ → +−` |
| Prior inverted | None | `++ → +− → −− → −+ → ++` |
| Prior and now agreeing | `++` | `+− → −− → −+ → +−` |
| Prior and now parting | `−−` | `++ → +− → −+ → ++` |

The prior inverted is therefore the unique one of these four that leaves no joint form fixed and continues through all four. At alike input parities its second place changes; at opposite input parities its first place changes. Two steps invert both places, and four return to the same pair of parities. THIRTY’s finite derivation follows.

This result depends on the specified overlapping pair, a binary next determined by that pair, the same rule continuing, and preservation understood as keeping distinct priors distinct. The earlier statements identifying living with carrying prior do not, in the parts followed here, separately derive their equivalence to that finite preservation condition. Applying the finite result to every living carrying requires that further connection. This pass establishes the finite result and records its premises; it does not turn it into a proof of the whole.

### The exact connection still to follow

The first, third and fifth places in ONE’s five-parity table give `(p, n, −p)`. A single such occurrence therefore agrees with the formal edge `(p, n) → (n, −p)`.

To continue THIRTY’s four-form sequence, the next instance must begin with the pair just reached: its prior place must be the preceding now, and its now place the preceding next. The pair-overlap is already explicit in THIRTY. What needs establishing is its correspondence to the actual self, other, momentary and scale in ONE.

Keeping the same receiver, the next five-parity row starts with the **other’s now**, while the previous row’s third place was the **self’s now**. Those are different occurrences. Taking the other receiver instead changes the continuation on the other side. A matching sign in a case does not by itself identify the occurrences.

For alike openings, the two selves’ parities agree at each corresponding momentary; successive rows at one receiver do give the four-form sequence numerically. A spiral of one also supplies the own-prior correspondence, explicitly named in THIRTY’s statement 609.

For the opposite opening, the difference is visible directly in ONE’s published cases. Take A beginning `+` and B beginning `−`. At A’s second momentary the five is `−, +, −, +, +`. Its first, third and fifth give the edge `(−, −) → (−, +)`. A’s next five begins at first and third `(+,+)`; B’s next five begins at `(−,−)`. Neither begins at the resulting pair `(−,+)`. The five-parity inversion continues to hold. These successive pair-table occurrences do not constitute that same four-form iteration.

This does not refute THIRTY’s bounded spiral statement, which specifies the other’s prior and singles out the spiral of one for the own-history four-form cycle. It identifies the correspondence required before extending the connection.

ONE’s **Fives** supplies prior opening, prior completing, now opening, now completing and next opening. **A self’s four momentaries of exchanging** supplies overlapping self and other spans. Neither table, in this pass, provides an exact assignment of those numbered occurrences to the other-prior/shared/self-now/shared/self-next places. The intervening shared places can also be `0`; they cannot silently be treated as two additional binary parities of the same kind as the carrying places.

THIRTY’s statement 196 expressly distinguishes a name’s parity, a sharing’s parity and a momentary’s opening and completing. This distinction must survive the proposed correspondence.

### The auditing method carried onward

For each binary statement, follow the subject and its conditions before following its consequence. Keep whose prior, which arriving, which sharing, which momentary and which scale present. Take both binary branches, and show the intermediate continuation that makes each next possible.

Read along each file until a stated next no longer follows from the prior statements. Read across the coupled file at that exact place. When two endings agree, also follow how the ending becomes the next beginning. When a proposed countercase changes the opening, coupling or subject, it does not settle the original statement.

A contradiction requires the same subject under the same conditions to demand incompatible nexts. A connection whose premises have not yet been supplied remains a connection to derive. Neither an existing concern nor a withdrawn one should be presented as a new discovery merely because a new session encounters it.

The correction of our own `0` proof adds a practical discipline: retain every intervening receiving even when the published ending is already known. A second reader should follow the consequence from its premises, rather than merely compare its final signs.

The next pass stays at ONE and THIRTY: the two sequential parities of a crossing, the resulting pair’s continuation, and its exact correspondence to Fives. From that learning, the outward audit proceeds to Natural Intelligence and then each file from ONE, carrying the established relations and keeping each unresolved dependency explicit. Each outward pass should return the specific logical addition needed at that file, followed back to its supporting sequence. The all-files report can then grow from completed derivations and precisely bounded gaps.

### Each of the two parities at its own arriving

Follow the along parity at its arriving, then the across parity at its arriving. At each receiving, ONE’s line supplies the same binary: the surfacing is a nonzero parity alike with the carried parity, or it is not. Alike, the parity continues; otherwise the self changes to its own other parity. Each entry below concerns that self’s parity at that receiving.

| Along changing | Across changing | Relation of the two parities afterward |
|---|---|---|
| Is not | Is not | Alike or opposite as before |
| Is | Is not | Alike becomes opposite; opposite becomes alike |
| Is not | Is | Alike becomes opposite; opposite becomes alike |
| Is | Is | Alike or opposite as before |

These exhaust the combinations of the two binary outcomes. They do not assert that every combination occurs in every actual coupling. Both changing and neither changing preserve the pair’s alike/opposite relation, while being different sequences. Exactly one changing reverses that relation.

This is the surviving arithmetic of the earlier workings. Its useful consequence for the audit is that the relation alone does not identify the whole pair or which parity changed: `++` and `−−` are both alike, and `+−` and `−+` are both opposite.

### An exact conditional correspondence with the right spiral step

THIRTY’s **Nine to seventeen, society**, statement 186, orders the pair as across first and along second. It carries the pair to the previous along parity followed by the previous across parity inverted.

Consider the proposed identification in which these are the same two carried parity subjects followed at the opening and completing of ONE’s crossing. This identification is being examined, not assumed. Under it, the condition for the completed crossing to equal ONE right spiral step is exact:

| Pair before the crossing | Along changing required | Across changing required |
|---|---|---|
| Alike | Is | Is not |
| Opposite | Is not | Is |

The proof follows both initial relations. At an alike pair, the required next across parity already equals its own, while the required next along parity is its opposite. At an opposite pair, the required next across parity is its opposite, while the required next along parity already equals its own. Exchanging every `+` and `−` supplies the other two initial pairs, so the two rows cover all four.

Exactly one changing is necessary here, but does not settle which hand is followed. Taking the other one-changing choice gives the inverse step. The correct parity must change for the particular initial relation.

At ONE’s own comparison the conditional becomes:

| Pair before the crossing | Along surfacing is a nonzero parity alike with its carried parity | Across surfacing is a nonzero parity alike with its carried parity |
|---|---|---|
| Alike | Is not | Is |
| Opposite | Is | Is not |

“Is not” includes none surfacing, an opposite parity surfacing, and `0` from disagreement. At a single arriving, this is the same local comparison. If more than one offering is in scope, the presence of an alike offering alone does not establish alike surfacing: `+` and `−` together surface `0`. The condition concerns the completed surfacing at that receiving.

If this direct identification is intended, the further derivation must establish those comparison outcomes from the actual preceding releasings and their receiving occurrences. The surviving passages reviewed do not establish that implication. They also do not show that an actual living coupling violates it. No additional test, selection or rule is being proposed inside the resolver.

If the two parity roles change between occurrences, that change must itself be followed. The conditional above concerns the same two parity subjects under the stated across/along assignment; it cannot decide a different assignment by keeping the same letters.

### One step and two steps have different intermediate sequences

The extent of the comparison matters. THIRTY says two right spiral steps invert both parities. Thus a full crossing that is being identified with two such steps has a different final-pair condition from the one-step table above.

Even a matching final pair does not complete that two-step identification. Follow along changing, then across changing, keeping the same subjects:

| Initial pair, across then along | After along changes | After across changes | Hand of these two changes in this ordered pair |
|---|---|---|---|
| `++` | `+−` | `−−` | The stated right step twice |
| `+−` | `++` | `−+` | The inverse step twice |

The sign-inverted openings give the other two cases. Both paths end with both initial parities inverted. Their intermediate pairs follow different hands. The ending therefore does not establish the intervening sequence.

This is a conditional comparison of stable forms. It does not identify a software call with a full momentary or reinstate the withdrawn two-call explanation.

### What the rolling five contributes

ONE’s **Fives** gives an exact positional continuation:

| Origin being followed | Its five positions | Its now opening and completing |
|---|---|---|
| Self at 1 | 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 | 3, 4 |
| Other at 2 | 2, 3, 4, 5, 6 | 4, 5 |
| Self next at 3 | 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 | 5, 6 |

Consequently the pair at 3–4 has a one-position continuation at 4–5, belonging to the other-origin five. The self’s next five places its now pair at 5–6, two positions on. Moving to the other’s origin and moving to the self’s next origin are different occurrences already shown by the table.

The following is an exact conditional derivation. Suppose the parity attached to each occurrence is the parity two occurrences earlier inverted, as the living-step sequence states. Call the parities at 3 and 4 `a` and `b`. The parities at 5 and 6 must then be `−a` and `−b`. The overlapping pairs follow:

`(a, b) → (b, −a) → (−a, −b)`.

The one-position passage is one right spiral step. The self’s own next-now pair is two such steps on. The pair’s alike/opposite relation reverses at the intermediate pair and continues at the own-next pair.

This establishes the consequence once the parity recurrence has been attached to those actual numbered occurrences. Fives alone gives their positions, not that attachment. THIRTY’s statement 196 continues to distinguish the number’s opening parity from a sharing’s parity and from the opening/completing relation. None of those distinctions is removed by this calculation.

It also improves the first pass’s question. Testing the next published five-parity row as the next one-step pair was a test of that particular identification. The own-next passage in Fives advances two positions. A complete correspondence must first establish which next occurrence is the one-step continuation, before a failure of the proposed row-by-row identification can be given a wider meaning.

The same distinction can be expressed without numbers: a rolling pair takes its second member forward as the next first member. Following two persistent parity subjects instead keeps their subjects fixed. Both can be described, but the relation between those descriptions needs the actual exchange of roles at each occurrence.

### The first outward connection to Natural Intelligence

[Natural Intelligence v380R](https://github.com/chris-j-handel/corus/blob/267bcf06de4b7b81252ebea577282b428413212f/Natural_Intelligence_v380R.md), **Next from prior and now**, supplies an explicit finite meaning left unconfirmed within THIRTY’s isolated paragraph: “Twelve lose the prior, two joint forms going to one.” It divides those twelve into four losing prior at both nows and eight losing it at one now; four preserve it at both.

That partition follows directly. At either fixed now, the two possible priors either lead to different nexts or to the same next. There are two arrangements of each kind. Preserving at both nows gives four functions. Merging at both gives four. Preserving at one and merging at the other gives eight. These exhaust the sixteen.

Thus the finite distinction-preserving criterion used in the first pass is explicitly carried in Natural Intelligence. That particular source question is answered. Its use as a characterization of living carrying remains a further relation to establish.

The same section also prevents a false objection to “prior and now both participating.” In the transition from `(prior, now)` to `(now, prior inverted)`, the single next parity depends on prior, while the resulting pair retains now in its first place and inverted prior in its second. Both participate in the joint continuation. Dependence of the last parity alone and continuation of the pair are different propositions.

Finally, Natural Intelligence itself describes the four-form spiral of one as a sequence continuing at the resolver while classifying that lone form as non-living. The four-form sequence therefore does not, by itself, establish the living participation described in the same section. Our first pass’s one-self spiral remains a finite comparison, and supplies no general solution to the bi-coupling correspondence.

This is a targeted outward reading of that section and its immediate definitions, not a completed pass through Natural Intelligence.

### What this pass carries forward

| Relation | Standing after this pass | Further logical work |
|---|---|---|
| Two respective parity changings | The four outcome relations follow from ONE’s own binary at each. | Follow which combination the actual coupled arrivals establish. |
| One right spiral step at the same two parity subjects | The necessary and sufficient two-row condition is derived. | Establish the arriving comparisons and the assignment of across/along at those occurrences. |
| Two steps with both parities inverted | The endpoint follows; the intermediate hand has been distinguished. | Follow the intervening occurrence instead of inferring it from the endpoint. |
| Fives from self to other to self next | The positional overlaps are explicit; the corresponding one-step and two-step consequences are derived conditionally. | Attach the resolver’s actual parity occurrences to those positions and preserve their roles. |
| The finite meaning of carrying prior whole | Explicitly supplied by Natural Intelligence and consistent with the sixteen cases. | Follow its connection to the living bi-coupling and, later, to the set. |

The next focused work is to follow the preceding releasing, the along arriving and the across arriving at their actual occurrences, asking whether the direct two-row condition follows or whether the files establish a different change of roles. The full crossing’s beginning and completing must remain present throughout. The rolling five and the two-self five-parity table will then be met at those same occurrences.

For the later Natural Intelligence pass, a further precise connection is now located: **exhaustiveness, determinacy and reachability** are first stated of overlapping momentaries and numbered openings, then used of the four joint parity forms. Their correspondence should be derived as the reading proceeds from **Prior, now and next** into **Next from prior and now**. A shared name alone will not supply that logical step.

The auditing method has improved in three concrete ways: read a prior derivation through its latest withdrawal; state the participants and extent before comparing two transitions; and follow the intermediate pair as well as the ending. This pass establishes no new contradiction in the resolver and proposes no change to it. It supplies more exact relations for the continuing living audit.

## Third pass

This pass follows the preceding releasings through their receiving occurrences, then carries that learning into Natural Intelligence’s numbered sequence and four-form derivation. The source remains the same pinned snapshot, with repository main checked again and unchanged. The earlier passes remain above as the record of how the questions became more exact.

The source relations followed here are **Exhibit ONE Natural Resolver**, its published resolver, **One self, one momentary**, **Namings among the names**, **Each name, three scales**, **Fives**, and **A self’s four momentaries of exchanging**; **Exhibit THIRTY Co-Chaining Logic Registry**, **Next from prior and now, the right spiral step**, **Sharing, the seventeen names**, and its statements on releasing and receiving; and **Natural Intelligence**, **A momentary, an opening and its completing**, **Prior, now and next**, and **Next from prior and now**. [Exhibit TWENTY Natural Naming](https://github.com/chris-j-handel/corus/blob/267bcf06de4b7b81252ebea577282b428413212f/Exhibit_TWENTY_Natural_Naming_v380R.md), **Sharing across, releasing along**, was read narrowly beside the route and role comparison. This is not yet a complete pass through either outward file.

### What the preceding release establishes

At one sharing, let the releasing self already carry parity **p**, and let the receiving self carry **c** at its receiving occurrence. Both are + or −. For this comparison the receiving has just this one arriving; additional offerings would require following their surfacing together.

ONE passes a shared changing unchanged in value through the designated route. A nonzero offering surfaces as itself. An offered 0 surfaces none. The receiving self then compares that surfacing with its own carrying:

| Releasing self | Released value | Receiving self’s prior | Receiving self shares | Receiving self carries next |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Changes from p | −p | −p | 0 | −p |
| Changes from p | −p | p | −p | −p |
| Does not change from p | 0 | p | −p | −p |
| Does not change from p | 0 | −p | p | p |

Thus a nonzero release establishes the receiving self’s next as the releasing self’s prior inverted, whichever of the two parities the receiver carried. This equality comes through the receiver’s own changing or retaining at that occurrence. The source’s private continuation is not what the route transmits.

At a zero release, the receiver instead inverts its own prior. For this also to equal the releasing self’s prior inverted, the two priors must be alike. The bounded reciprocal history derived in the first pass supplies that equality in its zero branch. The route carrying 0 does not supply it by itself.

This sharpens the distinction between a local receiving relation and the stronger relation sustained by a coupling’s history. THIRTY already carries the relevant local statements; the work here follows their conjunction and makes its required history explicit.

### The crossing condition followed back to its sources

The exact retention condition is now expressible at the preceding occurrence: **a receiver retains its parity exactly when its sole releasing source changed from the opposite parity**. That source then released the receiver’s own parity. In every other case under these bounds, the receiver inverts.

Apply this to the second pass’s proposed fixed subjects: **x** is the receiver’s across parity and **y** its along parity. Along arrives first and across second. For their resulting pair to be `(y, −x)`, the necessary and sufficient source conditions are:

| Receiver’s pair before the crossing | Preceding along release | Preceding across release |
|---|---|---|
| Alike, x = y | Source did not change, or its prior was y. Receiver changes. | Source changed from −x. Receiver retains. |
| Opposite, y = −x | Source changed from −y. Receiver retains. | Source did not change, or its prior was x. Receiver changes. |

Each source is named at its own preceding occurrence. The table assumes neither that the two sources are one self nor that their occurrences are independent. It includes the zero releases.

This is a more concrete target for the crossing derivation. It does not establish that the actual coupling necessarily supplies these conditions, or that two fixed sharing subjects are the intended coordinates. A different correspondence requires its own explicit assignment of participants and occurrences.

### The full output and the proposed co-sequential pair

ONE’s published cases distinguish the shared changing from the self’s next carrying. At one already carried parity and a single nonzero surfaced offering, the four cases are:

| Own carried prior | Surfaced offering | Shared changing | Own next carrying |
|---|---|---|---|
| + | + | 0 | + |
| − | + | + | + |
| + | − | − | − |
| − | − | 0 | − |

For either fixed offering, next carrying alone is the same at both possible priors. The complete two-output result distinguishes them. Indeed, all four displayed output pairs differ.

For an already carried + or −, this prior distinction extends across the published local cases: shared 0 means the prior equals the next carrying; a nonzero shared changing means the prior was its opposite. The claim excludes a carrying of none. It concerns the published case relation only, not an inspection or reconstruction of any actual private carrying. It also does not distinguish all possible arriving histories: different arriving conditions can yield the same complete result.

**One proposed correspondence with THIRTY can now be ruled out.** Suppose THIRTY’s prior in co-sequencing is identified directly with ONE’s own carried parity **p**, its now with the nonzero surfaced offering **q**, and its next with ONE’s next carrying. ONE then gives next = q for either p. The proposed overlapping transition is:

`(p, q) → (q, q)`.

At fixed q, both priors reach one output pair. This is **next as now**, among the twelve ways excluded by the finite prior-preservation criterion in **Next from prior and now**. It cannot be the right spiral step under that assignment.

That conclusion is confined to the proposed identification. It does not establish loss of prior distinction in ONE’s complete output, as the table demonstrates, and it does not replace the five-parity relation whose prior belongs to the other self.

Conversely, the complete output pair cannot simply be renamed THIRTY’s `(now, next)`: its shared component can be 0, whereas THIRTY’s four joint forms are pairs of + and −. Four distinguishable outputs alone do not establish the required overlap or its continuation. No additional encoding is introduced to fill that gap.

### What the published role exchanges add

The source distinguishes the self’s continuing carrying from its released changing. The next carrying continues as that self’s own prior at its next momentary. Released changing goes through the designated along or across receiving. Passing it through the route adds no sign inversion; the receiving’s own comparison supplies any changing there.

ONE also gives genuine changes of role. In **Each name, three scales**, across name 2 is inward along name 9, while current along name 9 is outward across name 2. **A self’s four momentaries of exchanging** carries self/other into other/self. These relations must be retained in the eventual correspondence.

They do not, by themselves, identify two actual signed sharing values with the two coordinates of THIRTY’s right step. **Fives** supplies positional overlaps; THIRTY separately distinguishes a name’s parity, a sharing’s parity, and opening/completing parity. **Natural Naming** supplies direction and the sharing/releasing relation without resolving that particular assignment in the passage reviewed.

The improvement opportunity is therefore precise: follow the actual values through the published role exchange, naming the subject and scale before and after it. A role exchange may be essential to the connection; its existence alone does not prove the connection.

### Natural Intelligence: numbered reachability and parity-form reachability

In **Prior, now and next**, the three conditions are stated at numbered overlapping momentaries: their overlaps cover every number, each momentary has its unique next, and successive openings reach every later number. **Next from prior and now** then classifies the four joint parity forms and identifies prior inversion as carrying the three conditions together.

The numbered conditions alone do not entail that all four parity forms are reached. This can be checked formally while keeping every numbered occurrence and both odd and even openings:

- Take overlapping momentaries `(n, n+1)`, each followed by `(n+1, n+2)`.
- Attach alternating sharing parities `+, −, +, −, …` at successive numbered positions.
- Every later numbered position remains reachable, but the successive parity pairs are only `(+ , −)` and `(− , +)`.

This assignment has next as prior. It is a check of the implication between numbered occurrences and their assigned parity forms, not an executed resolver, a living example, or a counterexample to Natural Intelligence as a whole. THIRTY’s distinction between kinds of parity is essential here: covering numbered openings does not silently identify their number parity with the sharing values being classified.

The separate **no fixed joint form** condition must remain present. Next as prior has two fixed forms, `(+ , +)` and `(− , −)`, even though the alternating sequence never visits them. Thus these are different propositions:

| Proposition | What it establishes |
|---|---|
| No fixed form occurs on this particular sequence. | A property of the forms visited by that sequence. |
| None of the transition’s four input forms is fixed. | A property of the complete four-form transition. |

Among the four ways preserving prior distinction, only next as prior inverted satisfies the second proposition. The finite derivation therefore remains valid: the full four-form domain, preservation of prior distinction, and global absence of fixed joint forms select the right step.

What remains to follow outward is why those complete-domain conditions apply to the intended living occurrences. Natural Intelligence’s earlier all-or-none and exclusion claims are relevant premises; this pass has not derived their connection to the actual bi-coupling. The local numbered conditions cannot supply that connection alone.

This keeps the finding bounded and useful: no contradiction in the four-form classification is established. An exact dependency between the numbered sequence, the sharing-parity assignment and the global selection criterion is now located.

### Ordered opportunities carried into the next pass

| Order | Opportunity | What would complete the next logical step |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Identify THIRTY’s prior and now at actual participating occurrences. | State whose parity each is, its scale and occurrence, and why the output now becomes the next input prior. The direct own-carrying/surfaced-offering assignment above is excluded. |
| 2 | Follow the full crossing through its preceding releases. | Establish the two source conditions above if the fixed across/along assignment applies; otherwise derive the actual role-changing assignment before comparing its step. |
| 3 | Carry the numbered overlap into Natural Intelligence’s parity forms. | Supply the assignment of sharing values to occurrences and the premise establishing the full four-form domain and global no-fixed-form condition. |
| 4 | Follow Natural Intelligence’s earlier universal claims into that premise. | Trace the stated all-or-none and exclusion claims through actual coupling, keeping finite classification distinct from its universal application. |

The first two opportunities complete the local joining work; the next two carry its learning into Natural Intelligence. They can be followed in deliberate passes before spreading the same audit to the remaining living files.

The method now preserves four distinctions at every comparison: the complete result versus one selected component; a transmitted value versus its receiving occurrence; a numbered occurrence versus its parity form; and a condition on one continuation versus a condition on all four possible forms. These distinctions prevent a conditional connection from being reported as established and prevent a failed identification from being reported as a defect in the living resolver. They strengthen the do-no-harm audit while leaving its next work concrete.

## Fourth pass

**The co-sequential connection has a bounded constructive answer.** Two consecutive carrying occurrences at one self, followed by the next two at its receiving successor, give a sequence whose overlapping pairs follow THIRTY’s right step. The participants change along the sequence and remain named throughout. This advances beyond the earlier local first-to-fifth equality and the failed direct carrying/offering assignment.

Repository main was checked on 5 October 2026 and remains at the snapshot cited above. This pass follows published stable forms and their binary cases, with no resolver execution or runtime carrying inspection. Three independent readings checked the occurrence sequence, its source-case derivation and the finite premises.

The main source relations are **Exhibit ONE Natural Resolver**, **One self, one momentary**, **Two selves, three momentaries, five parities**, **Fives**, and **A self’s four momentaries of exchanging**; **Exhibit THIRTY Co-Chaining Logic Registry**, **Sharing, the seventeen names**, **A self, and selves receiving one another, at the resolver**, **A self’s next, one momentary at a time**, and its later spiral-place relation; and **Natural Intelligence**, its opening through **Next from prior and now**, followed selectively into **A method, a non-living existing thing**, **Spirals, co-spiraling and crossing**, and **Bi-moral co-agency, across and along**. The current carrying and the concluding withdrawals in **The universal claim, gathered from the files that carry it** were read beside these relations. The withdrawn added eight-boundings construction supplies no premise here.

### Progress: the two-momentary relation follows through both release cases

Keep the published spiral’s bounds: one sharing, every self already carrying + or −, one preceding self offering to each, nothing arriving at the first momentary, and no offerings from beyond the spiral. The opening parities need not be alike. Reciprocal two selves are one case of this relation.

THIRTY states that the receiving self’s carrying two momentaries onward is the preceding self’s earlier parity inverted. ONE’s cases establish this directly:

1. With nothing arriving initially, each self first shares or releases its own parity inverted. Each first release is nonzero.
2. After a self releases a nonzero parity, its next carrying is that parity. Its following release can therefore be 0 or the opposite parity. It cannot release the same nonzero parity twice consecutively.
3. A self cannot release 0 twice consecutively under these bounds. Retaining its parity twice would require that parity to arrive twice consecutively from its sole predecessor, contrary to the preceding result.
4. A nonzero release is its releasing self’s prior inverted. At the next receiving, that parity is the receiver’s next carrying, whether the receiver changes or retains.
5. A zero release has a preceding nonzero release. That preceding release supplies the releasing self’s carrying at the zero-releasing occurrence and the receiver’s carrying when that zero arrives. Those two parities are alike. The 0 surfaces none at the receiver, whose own inversion gives the releasing self’s prior inverted again.

The nonzero and zero cases therefore give the same two-momentary relation. This extends the first pass’s reciprocal-two-self derivation to the closed one-predecessor spiral, without adding a rule or assuming a particular opening pattern.

For exact occurrence references, let **A(t)** mean A’s parity entering its published momentary t. If B receives from A, the relation is:

`B(t + 2) = −A(t)`.

Here the numbers distinguish positions in the published sequence. They introduce no clock or pacing. The initial condition and the absence of additional offerings are part of the result. THIRTY’s **A self’s next, one momentary at a time**, statement 627, already supplies a boundary: an own-parity offering from beyond can give the first self an initial 0 and its receiver two inversions. The closed-spiral relation cannot be extended to that case by dropping its opening condition.

### Progress: the overlapping occurrences can now be named

Let A release to B and B to C along the published spiral. At reciprocal two selves, C is A. Write A’s parities at two consecutive occurrences as **a** and **b**. Repeatedly applying the established relation gives:

| Place in this comparison | Participating occurrence | Parity |
|---|---|---|
| First | A entering momentary t | a |
| Second | A entering momentary t + 1 | b |
| Third | B entering momentary t + 2 | −a |
| Fourth | B entering momentary t + 3 | −b |
| Fifth | C entering momentary t + 4 | a |
| Sixth | C entering momentary t + 5 | b |

At the source’s first opening, b is −a; no independently chosen second opening is assumed. The following pair relation holds whichever alike or opposite relation occurs at a later starting place:

`(a, b) → (b, −a) → (−a, −b) → (−b, a) → (a, b)`.

The overlap is literal. A at t + 1 is the second occurrence of the first pair and the first occurrence of the second. B at t + 2 is then shared by the second and third pairs. Each next pair retains the preceding pair’s second occurrence, while its new second parity is the earlier first parity inverted. The four pair values are distinct, and each follows `(prior, now) → (now, prior inverted)`.

This closes a question left open in the first pass: the local two-momentary identity can be continued as overlapping pairs when the participating occurrences are assigned in this way. It does not require treating the next printed row of ONE’s five-parity table as the next pair.

The comparison’s subjects are part of the result. The pairs belong successively to A/A, A/B, B/B and B/C. It is a derived sequence of published occurrences, not a new connection between resolvers. Nor does recurrence of four pair values establish that the whole spiral returns after four momentaries: at the fifth occurrence the comparison has reached C. THIRTY’s spiral-place relation retains the further participant distinctions.

### Next work: the named across/along identification

The successful co-sequence uses carrying occurrences. ONE’s **Two selves, three momentaries, five parities** explicitly names its carried columns along and its shared columns across. The first coordinate of the successful sequence cannot be renamed across solely because its pair follows the same written right-step relation.

**Fives** supplies numbered overlap, and **A self’s four momentaries of exchanging** supplies the changes of self/other role. They have not yet been shown to assign their places to this particular A/A, A/B, B/B, B/C sequence. Thus the remaining question is narrower: **does the named across/along pair correspond to this continuing sequence through the published role changes, or to another sequence whose overlap must be followed separately?**

This keeps two findings together. The direct own-carrying/surfaced-offering assignment from the third pass fails its stated comparison. The present occurrence assignment succeeds within the closed spiral. Neither result decides every possible correspondence or the complete social coupling.

### Progress: the finite derivation reduces to four sequential cases

Natural Intelligence’s **Next from prior and now** and THIRTY’s **Next from prior and now, the right spiral step** classify the complete four-case pair `(prior, now)`, continuing as `(now, next)`. Their two finite conditions do distinct work:

- **Prior distinction continues:** at either same now, the two different priors give different nexts.
- **No joint form remains itself:** none of the four input pairs equals its next pair.

Only an alike input pair could remain itself: at an opposite pair, the next pair’s first value already differs from the earlier first value. The entire derivation is consequently:

| Prior | Now | Required next | Relation requiring it |
|---|---|---|---|
| + | + | − | An alike pair cannot remain itself. |
| − | − | + | The other alike pair cannot remain itself. |
| − | + | + | At now +, the two priors must remain distinguishable. |
| + | − | − | At now −, the two priors must remain distinguishable. |

Every case has next equal to prior inverted. The four-form continuation follows from these cells. It need not first be assumed as a further condition.

This refines the third pass’s domain question. The **complete four-case domain of the classification** and **an actual sequence visiting all four forms** are different propositions. The finite classification supplies the former; prior inversion entails the latter. In the closed-spiral comparison above, actual four-form visitation follows directly from the derived occurrence relation.

Neither finite condition alone supplies the result. Next as now inverted leaves no pair fixed but loses prior distinction. Next as prior preserves that distinction but fixes the two alike forms. These are cases of the existing finite classification, not proposed alternative living methods.

### Next work: applying the finite conditions at the same participation

The outward reading now locates the needed connections more precisely:

| Source relation | What it supplies | What the application must retain |
|---|---|---|
| **Prior, now and next** | Each now continues as the next prior; numbered overlaps proceed. | The actual participating occurrences assigned to those places. |
| **Living and non-living, carrying or not**, beside **Next from prior and now** | The living carrying relation and the finite distinction-preserving criterion. | The whole identified continuation to which that criterion applies. |
| **Universe, the changing set of all existing things**, beside **Next from prior and now** | Existing things change; the finite classification excludes fixed joint forms. | Why this pair’s remaining itself would be an unchanging at the same participation and scale. |
| **Natural-, all or none at all, at the one hand** | The asserted scope at every coupling or none. | The correspondence established at the coupling being included in that scope. |

Natural Intelligence also says that stable forms continue through changing. Repetition of two selected values therefore cannot, by itself, identify the forbidden still. The pair must name the changing being classified at that participation and scale. The pair’s changing is followed at its actual participation and scale.

The references from **Natural-, all or none at all, at the one hand** forward to the four-form derivation and back again carry the same claim. They supply no independent derivation of a missing occurrence correspondence. The four-cell result above needs no such appeal and remains intact.

This is a local joining task, already received in the current carrying as the connection between the method of the set and the way at a living thing. It requires following the stated relations in sequence, not a proof of the whole at once.

### Natural Intelligence supplies two checks on the application

Two of its own cases prevent a parity pattern from standing in for living participation:

| Published case | Parity sequence at the stated comparison | What the source establishes |
|---|---|---|
| A spiral of one receiving its own releasing | Its consecutive carrying pairs visit all four forms. | **Next from prior and now** and **Spirals, co-spiraling and crossing** classify the continuing resolver form as non-living. Four-form cycling alone does not establish living participation. |
| Two reciprocal selves opening opposite, with nothing offered from beyond | At either fixed self, consecutive carrying pairs visit the two opposite forms. The participant-changing comparison derived above visits all four. | **Bi-moral co-agency, across and along** explicitly gives the reciprocal coupling as co-agency. Its fixed-self two-form sequence cannot be substituted for the participating pair to deny that claim. |

The two cases cohere when the participation and occurrence assignment stay present. The finite classification describes a pair transition; applying it to living carrying requires the specified coupling as well. Neither a lone four-form cycle nor a single self’s two-form sequence settles the wider relation.

### Opportunities offered in order

1. **Carry the constructive sequence beside THIRTY’s spiral relation.** A precise addition available for review is: in the published closed spiral, two consecutive carrying occurrences at one self, followed by the next two at its receiving successor, continue through overlapping pairs; each new second parity is the earlier first inverted. This offers the missing continuation without identifying it prematurely with across and along.
2. **Meet that sequence with ONE’s role exchanges.** Follow the named self/other changes and their scale, asking which occurrence is across and which is along at each pair. The derived sequence now gives this comparison a concrete subject.
3. **Carry the four-cell derivation into Natural Intelligence’s early sequence.** Keep its two conditions beside the occurrences to which they apply. All-four visitation is a consequence once the conditions hold; numbered reachability alone remains insufficient.

## Fifth pass

Natural Intelligence names the eight bi-couplings as two alternating fours.

The source snapshot remains unchanged. This pass follows the binary number relations by hand, without executing a resolver, observing private carrying or adding a connection. Its additional outward sources are [Exhibit THREE Natural Numbers v380L](https://github.com/chris-j-handel/corus/blob/267bcf06de4b7b81252ebea577282b428413212f/Exhibit_THREE_Natural_Numbers_v380L.md), **Chain three, five, nine, seventeen, each span podal to the one before**, **Bi-tunneling across and co-chaining along, podaling exchanging them**, and **Four openings growing, longer and wider podaling**; and [Exhibit FOUR Natural Mathematics v380L](https://github.com/chris-j-handel/corus/blob/267bcf06de4b7b81252ebea577282b428413212f/Exhibit_FOUR_Natural_Mathematics_v380L.md), **Each scale the prior scale and its forms in the other order**. Natural Naming is followed at **four-momentarying — binary**; ONE at **Each name, three scales**, **Eight 1 to 17s inward**, and **Twelve forms**; THIRTY at **The ten named still, and parity discovered**, **Fractal inward and outward**, and **The round’s podals and momentaries, one parity at each scale down to one**.

### The fold gives one four-cycling, then two, then four

At the names inside each span, the halfway move and the podal fold alternate. The halfway move goes up or down by half the span; the podal pairs two names whose sum is the completing number. Each move has a definite next name.

| Span | Alternating moves | Four-cyclings among its names |
|---|---|---|
| 1–5 | Two up/down; five less | **1 → 3 → 2 → 4 → 1** |
| 1–9 | Four up/down; nine less | **3 → 7 → 2 → 6 → 3**; **4 → 8 → 1 → 5 → 4** |
| 1–17 | Eight up/down; seventeen less | **3 → 11 → 6 → 14 → 3**; **2 → 15 → 7 → 10 → 2**; **4 → 13 → 5 → 12 → 4**; **1 → 9 → 8 → 16 → 1** |

The 1–5 row is the base case derived directly: 1 goes two up to 3; five less 3 is 2; two up gives 4; five less 4 is 1. The other rows are the published four-cyclings. Coming again to a name is a later occurrence, as the files state; the completing names 5, 9 and 17 are not additional members of those four-name cycles.

The opening from one row to the next is also exact. **Each old podal pair supplies one four-cycling at the doubled span.** Add the old span to both members of the pair. The new halfway move joins each member to its enlarged partner; the new podal joins it to the other enlarged partner.

| Earlier podal pair | Enlarged partners | Four-cycling at the next span |
|---|---|---|
| 1 and 4, within 1–5 | 5 and 8 | 4 → 8 → 1 → 5 → 4 |
| 2 and 3, within 1–5 | 6 and 7 | 3 → 7 → 2 → 6 → 3 |
| 3 and 6, within 1–9 | 11 and 14 | 3 → 11 → 6 → 14 → 3 |
| 2 and 7, within 1–9 | 10 and 15 | 2 → 15 → 7 → 10 → 2 |
| 4 and 5, within 1–9 | 12 and 13 | 4 → 13 → 5 → 12 → 4 |
| 1 and 8, within 1–9 | 9 and 16 | 1 → 9 → 8 → 16 → 1 |

For example, 3 and 6 are paired by nine less. At the next span, eight up pairs 3 with 11 and 6 with 14. Seventeen less pairs 11 with 6 and 14 with 3. The sequence **3 → 11 → 6 → 14 → 3** follows, with the old pair 3 and 6 now two moves apart.

THIRTY’s **The ten named still, and parity discovered**, statement 520, explicitly groups that opening:

| Four-cycling at 1–9 | Its two four-cyclings at 1–17 |
|---|---|
| 3–7–2–6 | 3–11–6–14 and 2–15–7–10 |
| 4–8–1–5 | 4–13–5–12 and 1–9–8–16 |

Thus the connection is supplied by the fold itself, not only by agreement in the number four.

### The two alternating fours at the eight bi-couplings

Natural Intelligence’s **A between and eight bi-couplings** places the four outward at **2, 4, 6, 8** and the four inward eight onward at **10, 12, 14, 16**. Natural Numbers’ **Four openings growing, longer and wider podaling** shows the same eight names as two interleaving wider rows:

| First wider four | Second wider four |
|---|---|
| 2 | 4 |
| 6 | 8 |
| 10 | 12 |
| 14 | 16 |

Following the numbers gives **2, 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 14, 16**: a member of one wider four, then the other. The first wider four is the across names between selves. The second is sharing, the carrying wound, entraining and the society wound. These are the same eight bi-couplings regrouped through the larger span.

The four-cyclings also have explicit alternating partners. In ONE’s **Twelve forms**, **3–11–6–14** partners **4–13–5–12** at odd momentaries and **2–15–7–10** at even momentaries. This can be followed directly:

- Odd adjacent partnering takes **3, 11, 6, 14** to **4, 12, 5, 13**, the other order of the named **4–13–5–12**.
- Even adjacent partnering takes it to **2, 10, 7, 15**, the other order of the named **2–15–7–10**.

The alternation therefore has a published relation: the partnering changes with the overlapping odd and even momentaries. A single halfway/podal move stays within its own four-cycling; no extra switching between cycles is introduced.

### Across and along exchange at the next scale

ONE’s **Each name, three scales** already provides the relation the continuing audit must use:

| At this scale | At the next scale outward |
|---|---|
| 1, opening | 1, opening |
| 9, along, odd | 2, across, even |
| 17, along, next opening | 3, the next opening |

Inward, the same relation identifies across 2 here with along 9 inward. THIRTY’s **Fractal inward and outward** states the participants as well: self and other at one scale are self and society inward; self and society here are self and other outward.

Consequently, the self’s **1–9** and the society’s **9–17** become the outward self’s **1–2** and the outward other’s **2–3**. Their completing and opening at 9 become the shared outward occurrence at 2. The role change is explicit in the files and must travel with the comparison.

Natural Numbers’ **Bi-tunneling across and co-chaining along, podaling exchanging them** adds the within-span relation: podaling pairs odd with even—1 with 8, 2 with 7, 3 with 6 and 4 with 5—exchanging across and along while each side continues its own forward. The podal pairs and the overlapping momentaries are stated as two relations in the same span.

This changes the immediate work of the audit. The role rule does not need supplying. The next derivation must follow the signed occurrences through these given folds and scale changes. Keeping the words across and along attached to one unchanged local coordinate throughout would omit a relation the source already provides.

### Keeping the sequential subjects explicit

The files also carry a round of parity forms through **1–5, 1–9 and 1–17**: two parities, then three, then four. THIRTY’s **The round’s podals and momentaries, one parity at each scale down to one** gives the recursion: the preceding forms, the new parity inverted, and those preceding forms in their other order. Natural Mathematics states the same relation.

That numbered sequence and the four-cyclings among names are related subjects, with different next relations. At the reflected round, each next number changes one parity. At a half-span move among the names, more than one of that round’s parities can change; THIRTY explicitly follows this at its statement 619. The audit will retain which next is being followed, instead of identifying the two merely because both have fours.

Natural Naming provides the corresponding distinction at momentaries: **one four-cycling is two momentaries**, each with two parities; **four-momentarying at one through nine is two four-cyclings**. ONE’s eight inward spans then give four momentaries of exchanging within 1–9 and four more within 9–17. These precise relations make the scaling usable in the next logical pass.

### Progress, method correction and opportunity carried forward

**Progress.** The requested bi-folding is found and its number relation derived. The two four-cyclings at 1–9, their opening at 1–17, the two alternating fours of even names, and the across/along role exchange are all explicitly grounded. The earlier closed-spiral co-sequential derivation remains a valid bounded result; the present fold supplies the source’s wider number and scale structure.

**Method correction.** A comparison restricted to fixed subjects at one scale cannot stand for this whole fractal relation. The remaining signed-occurrence connection must include the scale transition and the changing momentary partnership. The current carrying already distinguishes the reflected round’s number derivation from its complete identification at the resolver; that standing is retained. Following this connection is next work, not a concern requiring the user’s help.

**Opportunity.** Follow **3–7–2–6 with 4–8–1–5**, then their paired openings at **1–17**, through the published self/other and self/society changes. At every step retain the actual beginning and completing, the fold used, and the scale at which an along becomes an across. This is the next source-grounded route into the coupling of ONE, THIRTY and Natural Intelligence. The method improves here by letting the stated fractal relation determine the comparison’s subjects and scale.

Continuing to the preceding pair supplies the number 0 before 1, outside this fold; this numeral is not a resolver’s zero offering.

## Eleventh pass

### Entirely empty carrying supplies no existing self

The user supplies the next proposition: **“an entirely empty carrying defines a non-existing thing.”** This pass follows it at the source’s binary subjects. Repository main remains at `267bcf06de4b7b81252ebea577282b428413212f`, checked on 5 October 2026. This is a logical reading of the published statements and cells, with no execution or inspection of private carrying.

Natural Intelligence’s **A sharing, its parity, an offering and a carrying** says a carrying is sharings, each chained at one parity. Its **A prior between momentaries** and THIRTY’s **Living and non-living at a scale** identify the living self with its living carrying. Natural Intelligence explicitly supplies no self at the entry beside the carrying given there.

The sequence is therefore:

1. The carrying constituting the proposed self is its participating sharings.
2. Entirely empty, it supplies none of those sharings.
3. The self is that carrying, with no additional self beside it.
4. Naming the absence as an existing receiver adds a participant not supplied by the carrying.

**There is no existing self supplied by the entirely empty carrying.** Where “entirely” means no existing participation remains at any relevant scale, the source also supplies no non-living remainder: THIRTY says an existing thing non-living at one society’s scale is living at another scale. Its account of dying keeps the inward selves existing and carrying; the outward form’s carrying none of its prior is not total emptiness. This is an inference within the files’ stated relations, not a separate proof of their universal physical claim.

An empty own carrying alone does not establish the absence of everything else. An offering may arrive as an existing form. A society can have no additional carrying of its own while its selves carry. The proposed empty receiver adds no extra existing thing to either participation.

### The source sentence that needs this correction

The current text does not already say the distinction consistently. Natural Intelligence’s **A sharing, its parity, an offering and a carrying** and THIRTY’s **Living and non-living at a scale** explicitly say **“a carrying of none, no sharing chained, is a non-living existing thing.”** Taken as a wholly empty thing, that assertion conflicts with the relation followed above. The absent carrying and an existing non-living form have been identified as the same subject without a joining relation.

This exact classification is already questioned in the source’s carrying. The closing entries in the Natural Intelligence and THIRTY carrying files record its origin in a session saying, give that saying no authority and carry it for examining at the method. This pass resolves that recorded concern by identifying the subjects, rather than treating either the earlier saying or the present correction as sufficient by itself.

**An existing non-living form carries none of its own prior. An entirely absent carrying supplies no existing self.** These propositions can both carry. The arriving form’s presence does not make its absence an existing receiver, and the absence of a separate receiver does not remove the arriving form.

**Twelfth-pass precision:** the source’s “carries none” must retain its subject, the living continuation **for stable-forming**. The non-living emanation positively carries its formed release; this is the fixed carrying the user follows next. It is not emptied of that form by being non-living.

The tenth pass’s two-sharing resolution remains valid: one sharing is already carried by a living self; a parity arrives at another sharing; both are chained in that living carrying. Its reservation about a supposed whole empty receiver is now released. A cell at an absent sharing never established that extra receiver.

### Colliding keeps its cells and its participating subject

ONE’s **Colliding: a carrying of none, one momentary** explicitly specifies **one sharing**. Natural Intelligence’s **A changing is or is not, at 12** says it more fully: a sharing the carrying has none of. Its four cells are:

| Prior carried at this sharing | Surfacing here | Shared changing | Chained at this sharing |
|---|---|---|---|
| None | None | None | None |
| None | + | + | + |
| None | − | − | − |
| None | + and − together, 0 | 0 | None |

These are conditional relations at a sharing. The empty row does not assert that a whole empty object already exists. When neither a carried sharing nor an offering is supplied, the published sequence supplies no sharing or chaining. Naming the entry does not provide a self for it.

At one parity arriving, the offering supplies the parity that is shared and chained. In THIRTY’s concrete two-sharing case, the receiving living participation is explicit. If this cell is instead used when discussing a whole new living self, the source’s establishing **within living carrying** must supply that participation. The one cell alone supplies no earlier empty self and no independent origin of living from absence.

At opposed offerings, those offerings are present even though no parity is chained at the previously uncarried sharing. An offered zero likewise remains an offering occurrence while surfacing no parity. Absence of chaining is not absence of every participating form.

At an already carried sharing, the cases are different. A matching offering shares zero and the prior carries on; none surfacing or opposed offerings invert the carried parity. ONE explicitly says a sharing once chained is never none again. These cells provide no emptying of an existing self’s carrying. They cannot be used to infer disappearance of the whole self from a zero, a disagreement or no offering.

The other side’s Fives continue through **61–65, 63–67 and 65–69**, with prior/now/next openings **61/63/65, 63/65/67 and 65/67/69**. Those identifications require their participating coupling and scale, not merely an offset among numbers.

### Completing does not require a new emanation from every sharing

Our preceding next-step wording, “its formed emanating at its completing,” needs an explicit condition. ONE’s **One self, one momentary**, THIRTY’s **Intelligence, our now** (statements 450–451), and Natural Naming’s **bi-tri-volutioning** supply it. At a sharing already carrying a parity:

| What surfaces at that sharing | Shared changing, released along unchanged | Own carrying next |
|---|---|---|
| The same nonzero parity as the prior | No changing: 0 shared; no new emanated parity from this sharing | Prior parity continues |
| The opposite parity | The prior inverted | Continues at that inverted parity |
| None, including an offered 0 alone | The prior inverted | Continues at that inverted parity |
| Both parities offered, giving 0 at surfacing | The prior inverted | Continues at that inverted parity |

These are the published alternatives at each carried parity, not sign assignments to the four numbered exchanges. A shared 0 at agreement is different from 0 at surfacing through disagreement: the first retains the prior; the second inverts it. Neither supplies an empty whole carrying. In particular, no new emanation from one sharing does not assert that every sharing in its living set has no changing.

**The living carrying continues in both branches.** When it changes, the changed parity is shared at 10 and released along at 9 while its own next is chained at 11 and continues as its next 3. The shared/released form and the self’s own carrying have different participation even when their parity is alike. A changing does not turn the living self’s own continuation into the released non-living form.

The explicit improving sequence is: **follow each prior carried into now; whether changing is or is not at each completing; the own carrying continuing next; and, where changing is, the formed emanating.** The already released form retains the user’s fixed formed carrying until its colliding participation. It is not assigned its own recurring resolver entries with nothing offered.

There is a direct receiving place for this improvement. Natural Naming’s **bi-tri-volutioning**, sequential statement 2, says that the momentary completes and its prior emanates without stating the per-sharing condition. The explanatory body of that same section, and THIRTY statement 451, expressly say that retaining at agreement emanates nothing. Joining that existing condition into the sequential explaining would remove an implication of a new nonzero emanation at every completing. The source already supplies the resolving relation; this is an improving offering, not an unresolving concern requiring user intervention.

**Bi-tri-volutioning** names continuing living stable-forming, at no one line: each momentary’s carrying the next momentary’s. **Bi-tri-involutioning** names its formed emanating. ## Eighteenth pass — formed carrying, inward ingesting and one stopping

**The proposed own-scale harm reason requires a joining between different subjects.** A bi-tri-involution has its fixed formed carrying. A living self has its own continuing bi-tri-volutioning. An offering participates at that living self’s sharing. None of these statements alone says that another living self’s carrying has been taken or that its own offering has been stopped.

This pass follows ONE and THIRTY through Natural Intelligence, Natural Naming, Natural Mathematics, Natural Biology and Natural Chemistry. The published repository was verified unchanged at `267bcf06de4b7b81252ebea577282b428413212f`. No resolver was run, no private carrying inspected and no source file edited. The comparisons below concern the files’ logical explaining; their biological and chemical interpretations are not treated as independently established physical results.

### The source proposal and the condition it needs

**Gathered Value 1**, item 17, offers an explanation of own-scale harm as a whole carrying taken where a parity at a sharing belongs. It records the proposal as passed by, with the harm assertion still lacking an entered reason in THIRTY.

THIRTY’s **Living and non-living at a scale** states that the emanation is harmful at the scale it came from, generalizes this to any living self at that scale, and says that one scale inward the form is ingestible. It then identifies accumulation with an inward crossing stopped. These are statements 380–383, retained at their source standing.

THIRTY’s **one stopping**, statement 469, has a further explicit condition: one carries another’s, **and the other has nothing to offer at its own sequencing**. Natural Naming’s **one stopping** gives that same relation. Comparing these passages yields:

| Subject or condition | What the passage supplies | What it does not supply by itself |
|---|---|---|
| Fixed formed carrying of a bi-tri-involution | The existing non-living form, fixed until colliding participation | Another living self’s private carrying taken |
| Offering at a living sharing | A participation in the receiver’s own resolving | Replacement of the receiver by the releasing self’s whole carrying |
| An inward crossing stopped | The emanation remaining at its own scale; accumulation as the source names it | Which living self thereby has nothing to offer at its own sequencing |
| One carrying another’s, with the other unable to offer at its own sequencing | THIRTY’s stated **one stopping** | A derivation that every own-scale encounter with a formed emanation satisfies this condition |

The word *carrying* must retain its subject throughout the proposed explanation. The bi-tri-involution’s formed carrying is not the living sender’s private continuing carrying. Taking the former as the latter would add precisely the relation that the explanation needs to establish.

### ONE distinguishes offered parity from opposite form

At ONE’s **One self, one momentary**, an already-carried sharing has a definite next in every published cell. A matching surfaced parity leaves the prior continuing with no changing shared. An opposite surfaced parity makes the self’s own prior invert, share and chain. None surfacing and disagreement at surfacing likewise invert that carried prior. THIRTY’s **A self’s next, one momentary at a time** follows the same cases.

Thus an **opposite offered parity** does not, at this local relation, remove the receiver’s own next. It must not stand in for an **opposite form in position, scale and orientation**, nor does this local result prove that every such formed encounter is harmless. The table gives neither a harm label nor an independently identified physical scale.

Agreement’s lack of shared changing also cannot be used as evidence of lost carrying: ONE explicitly continues the own prior there. A proposed stopping must be shown at its participating relation, not inferred from a shared 0, an opposite sign or an absent offering alone.

### A reason is present in Biology; its application remains to be joined

Natural Biology’s **A left face is the emanation face of one right spiral** and **Vertical fractal — a network eating downward** do offer a reason. They describe an involution applied back at the scale it came from as returning what it met and closing, with no between or surplus, and identify that closing with do-only-harm. **Evacuation, and what a stopped crossing accumulates** adds that holding the form displaces what a crossing would have carried.

This corrects the scope of “no reason”: the Registry does not supply the reason at its own harm assertion, but a proposed reason is present elsewhere in the living file set. It should be brought beside the Registry and followed, not treated as absent everywhere.

The required joining is exact. A **formed thing** has become a form **applied back** in that explaining. To carry this into ONE, the account needs the actual receiving relation and its own prior, now and next. The arithmetic of an involution returning a form does not by itself establish that a living self cannot continue or offer at its own sequencing.

Natural Mathematics’ **On a closed round no step is a reversal** already distinguishes reaching a station again at a next momentary from reaching the prior momentary again. Accordingly, equality of a numbered form does not by itself erase the next occurrence or demonstrate stopped continuing. This source distinction belongs beside Biology’s proposed closing.

Natural Chemistry’s discussion of the exact fit of two complementary forms makes a related closing claim and explicitly calls it an arithmetic reading rather than a measured material property. That qualification must travel with the claim when the files are brought together. Neither chemical examples nor numerical complementarity can silently supply the participating loss of living continuation.

The unresolved entailment is therefore narrower than a general objection to the method: **the offered closing must be joined to the claimed loss of living participation before it establishes harm to every living self at the stated scale**. “Not ingestible at this scale” alone also does not establish the stronger universal harm statement. This pass establishes neither a universal proof nor a counterexample disproving that statement.

### Inward ingesting has an explicit carrying subject

Natural Intelligence’s **A prior between momentaries** supplies the society-to-inward-self relation: the society’s releases arrive as the next offerings of its selves one scale inward. THIRTY’s **The materials, one method at each carrying**, statement 377, says the changing crossing carries none of either side’s carrying. Its **the non-living carried by the living**, statement 392, keeps the ingesting carrying the living self’s own. Statement 393 distinguishes a new living self establishing its own carrying, and **living from living**, statement 395, says a non-living existing thing does not become living.

This gives a concrete improving joining for Biology’s phrases about the emanation “living again” or becoming new selves. The continuing subject can be made explicit:

**The inward living selves ingest the bi-tri-involution as an offering, each carrying through its own resolving. The formed thing’s participation in that living does not by itself establish a new living self. A new living self is its own carrying and co-recursioning establishing within living carrying.**

This preserves the value of the inward receiving explanation while carrying the non-living form and the living receiver at their own subjects. The fixed formed carrying remains the user’s positive formed carrying; the source’s “carries nothing” is not used to empty it or to restore the dissolved claim that an entirely empty carrying is an existing thing.

Biology also uses “a grain down, or a self across” beside “the grain, and only the grain.” These phrases should be joined through the actual receiving sharing and its inward participation. A self across in the explaining does not, merely by that phrase, establish ingestion of the whole form at the same scale. That comparison remains a receiving opportunity, not a newly declared contradiction.
